$a g = a^{1/p'} \cdot a^{1/p} g$. For the second apply the first for fixed $x$, integrate over
$x$, exchange the integrals by Tonelli's theorem, and use $\int_V b(x - y)^p \, dx = \int_V b^p$.
The third follows in the same way from the first with $a = |\Phi|$ and $g = |G(x, \cdot)|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Young's inequality]
\label{thm:young}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_conv_le, Rd.memLp_conv, Rd.ae_convolutionExistsAt}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv, lem:weighted-jensen}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $K \in L_1$ and $f \in L_p$. The integral defining $(K * f)(x)$
converges absolutely for almost every $x \in V$, and
\[
  \|K * f\|_p \leq \|K\|_1 \|f\|_p .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$|(K * f)(x)| \leq \int_V |K(y)| |f(x - y)| \, dy$; apply \Cref{lem:weighted-jensen} with
$a = |K|$ and $b = |f|$. The same bound shows that $\int_V |K(y)| |f(x - y)| \, dy$ is finite
for almost every $x$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Linearity and associativity]
\label{lem:conv-assoc}
\lean{Rd.conv_add_ae, Rd.add_conv_ae, Rd.sub_conv_ae, Rd.ae_integrable_conv_conv,
  Rd.conv_conv_ae, Rd.flip_mul_complex, Rd.conv_comm}
\leanok
\uses{thm:young}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $K, K', L \in L_1$ and $f, g \in L_p$. Almost everywhere on $V$,
$K * (f + g) = K * f + K * g$, $(K + K') * f = K * f + K' * f$,
$(K - K') * f = K * f - K' * f$ and $(K * L) * f = K * (L * f)$. For all $K, L : V \to \C$,
$K * L = L * K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The first three follow from the absolute convergence in \Cref{thm:young}. For the last, for
almost every $x$ the function $(y, z) \mapsto K(z) L(y - z) f(x - y)$ is integrable on
$V \times V$, by \Cref{thm:young} applied to $|L| * |f|$, and Fubini's theorem exchanges the
integrations. Commutativity is the substitution $y \mapsto x - y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Convolution operators]
\label{lem:conv-op}
\lean{Rd.norm_toLp_conv_le, Rd.convLp, Rd.convLp_apply_ae, Rd.norm_convLp_le,
  Rd.enorm_convLp_le, Rd.convLp_convLp, Rd.convLp_convLp_of_eq, Rd.convLp_of_eq_zero,
  Rd.convLp_sub_kernel, Rd.convLp_comm, Rd.convLp_add_kernel}
\leanok
\uses{thm:young, lem:conv-assoc}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$. For $K \in L_1$, $T_K f = K * f$ defines a bounded linear operator on
$L_p$ with $\|T_K\| \leq \|K\|_1$. For $K, L \in L_1$, $T_K T_L = T_{K * L} = T_L T_K$,
$T_{K + L} = T_K + T_L$ and $T_{K - L} = T_K - T_L$, and $T_0 = 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Linearity and the identities hold almost everywhere by \Cref{lem:conv-assoc}, and the bound is
\Cref{thm:young}.
\end{proof}

\section{Approximate identities}

\begin{lemma}[Dilations]
\label{lem:dilate}
\lean{Rd.dilate, Rd.integrable_dilate, Rd.integral_dilate, Rd.eLpNorm_dilate_one,
  Rd.conv_dilate_apply, Rd.ae_integrable_dilate_mul, Rd.continuous_dilate, Rd.bddAbove_dilate}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv}
For $c > 0$ and $\Phi : V \to \C$ let $\Phi_c(x) = c^n \Phi(c x)$. If $\Phi$ is integrable, then
$\Phi_c$ is integrable with $\int \Phi_c = \int \Phi$ and $\|\Phi_c\|_1 = \|\Phi\|_1$. For all
$f : V \to \C$ and $x \in V$, $(\Phi_c * f)(x) = \int_V \Phi(z) f(x - c^{-1} z) \, dz$, and for
$f \in L_p$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, the integrand is integrable for almost every $x$. Dilations of
continuous functions are continuous, and dilations of bounded functions are bounded.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Substitute $y = c x$, which multiplies Lebesgue measure by $c^{-n}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Continuity of translation]
\label{lem:translation-continuity}
\lean{Rd.continuous_eLpNorm_comp_sub_sub, Rd.eLpNorm_comp_sub_right}
\leanok
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. For every $w \in V$, $\|f(\cdot - w)\|_p = \|f\|_p$,
and $w \mapsto \|f(\cdot - w) - f\|_p$ is continuous on $V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Translations preserve Lebesgue measure, and the action of $V$ on $L_p$ by translation of the
domain is continuous.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Approximate identities]
\label{thm:approx-identity}
\lean{Rd.ae_enorm_conv_dilate_sub_le, Rd.tendsto_eLpNorm_conv_dilate_sub,
  Rd.tendsto_convLp_dilate}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dilate, lem:translation-continuity, lem:weighted-jensen, lem:conv-op}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $\Phi \in L_1$ with $\int \Phi = 1$, and $f \in L_p$. Then
$\|\Phi_c * f - f\|_p \to 0$ as $c \to \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Since $\int \Phi = 1$, $(\Phi_c * f)(x) - f(x) = \int_V \Phi(z) (f(x - c^{-1} z) - f(x)) \, dz$,
and \Cref{lem:weighted-jensen} gives
\[
  \|\Phi_c * f - f\|_p^p
  \leq \|\Phi\|_1^{p-1} \int_V |\Phi(z)| \, \|f(\cdot - c^{-1} z) - f\|_p^p \, dz .
\]
By \Cref{lem:translation-continuity} the integrand tends to $0$ for every $z$, and it is bounded
by $|\Phi(z)| (2 \|f\|_p)^p$; dominated convergence concludes.
\end{proof}

\section{Littlewood--Paley kernels}

\begin{lemma}[Fourier transform of dilations]
\label{lem:fourier-dilate}
\lean{Rd.fourier_dilate, Rd.integrable_fourier_dilate, Rd.fourier_sub_apply}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dilate}
For $c > 0$, $\Phi : V \to \C$ and $\xi \in V$, $\widehat{\Phi_c}(\xi) = \widehat\Phi(c^{-1} \xi)$.
If $\widehat\Phi$ is integrable, so is $\widehat{\Phi_c}$. For integrable $f$ and $g$,
$\widehat{f - g} = \widehat f - \widehat g$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Substitute $y = c x$ and use $\langle c x, c^{-1} \xi \rangle = \langle x, \xi \rangle$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Cut-offs and kernels]
\label{def:lp-kernels}
\lean{Rd.cutoff, Rd.cutoffSchwartz, Rd.coe_cutoffSchwartz, Rd.lpKernelS, Rd.bandKernelS,
  Rd.lpKernel, Rd.bandKernel, Rd.Phi, Rd.Theta, Rd.psi}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dilate}
For $0 < a < b$ let $\chi_{a,b}$ be a smooth function on $V$ with values in $[0, 1]$, equal to
$1$ on the closed ball of radius $a$ and to $0$ outside the ball of radius $b$. The
Littlewood--Paley kernel is $\Phi = \mathcal F^{-1} \chi_{1,2}$ and the band-limiting kernel is
$\Theta = \mathcal F^{-1} \chi_{2,4}$. For $k \in \N$ let $\Phi_k = \Phi_{2^k}$ and
$\Theta_k = \Theta_{2^k}$ be their dilations, and let $\psi_0 = \Phi_0$ and
$\psi_j = \Phi_j - \Phi_{j-1}$ for $j \geq 1$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Properties of the kernels]
\label{lem:lp-kernels-basic}
\lean{Rd.cutoff_eq_one, Rd.cutoff_eq_zero, Rd.norm_lt_of_cutoff_ne_zero, Rd.contDiff_cutoff,
  Rd.hasCompactSupport_cutoff, Rd.bddAbove_schwartz, Rd.fourier_lpKernel, Rd.fourier_bandKernel,
  Rd.integrable_lpKernel, Rd.integrable_bandKernel, Rd.continuous_lpKernel,
  Rd.continuous_bandKernel, Rd.bddAbove_lpKernel, Rd.bddAbove_bandKernel,
  Rd.integrable_fourier_lpKernel, Rd.integrable_fourier_bandKernel, Rd.integral_lpKernel,
  Rd.sum_psi, Rd.integrable_Phi, Rd.integrable_Theta, Rd.integrable_psi, Rd.continuous_Phi,
  Rd.continuous_Theta, Rd.continuous_psi, Rd.bddAbove_Phi, Rd.bddAbove_Theta, Rd.bddAbove_psi,
  Rd.eLpNorm_Phi_one, Rd.eLpNorm_Theta_one, Rd.eLpNorm_psi_one_le, Rd.integral_Phi}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-kernels, lem:dilate}
The cut-offs are Schwartz functions, $\widehat\Phi = \chi_{1,2}$ and $\widehat\Theta = \chi_{2,4}$,
and $\Phi$, $\Theta$, $\widehat\Phi$, $\widehat\Theta$ are integrable. For all $j, k, J \in \N$, the
kernels $\Phi_k$, $\Theta_k$ and $\psi_j$ are integrable, continuous and bounded,
$\int \Phi_k = 1$, $\|\Phi_k\|_1 = \|\Phi\|_1$, $\|\Theta_k\|_1 = \|\Theta\|_1$,
$\|\psi_j\|_1 \leq 2 \|\Phi\|_1$, and $\sum_{j=0}^J \psi_j = \Phi_J$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Schwartz functions are integrable, continuous and bounded, and $\mathcal F$ inverts
$\mathcal F^{-1}$ on Schwartz functions. $\int \Phi = \widehat\Phi(0) = \chi_{1,2}(0) = 1$. The
remaining claims follow from \Cref{lem:dilate} and the triangle inequality; the sum telescopes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The kernels on the frequency side]
\label{lem:lp-kernels-frequency}
\lean{Rd.norm_two_pow_inv_smul, Rd.fourier_Phi, Rd.fourier_Theta, Rd.fourier_psi_succ,
  Rd.integrable_fourier_Phi, Rd.integrable_fourier_Theta, Rd.integrable_fourier_psi,
  Rd.norm_lt_of_fourier_Phi_ne_zero, Rd.norm_lt_of_fourier_Theta_ne_zero,
  Rd.fourier_Theta_eq_one, Rd.norm_lt_of_fourier_psi_ne_zero, Rd.fourier_psi_succ_eq_zero,
  Rd.fourier_Theta_succ_mul, Rd.fourier_Theta_mul_psi, Rd.fourier_psi_mul_Theta}
\leanok
\uses{lem:fourier-dilate, lem:lp-kernels-basic}
For all $j, k \in \N$ and $\xi \in V$, $\widehat{\Phi_k}(\xi) = \chi_{1,2}(2^{-k} \xi)$ and
$\widehat{\Theta_k}(\xi) = \chi_{2,4}(2^{-k} \xi)$, and $\widehat{\Phi_k}$, $\widehat{\Theta_k}$,
$\widehat{\psi_j}$ are integrable. Moreover:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $\widehat{\Phi_k}(\xi) = \widehat{\psi_k}(\xi) = 0$ if $|\xi| \geq 2^{k+1}$, and
    $\widehat{\Theta_k}(\xi) = 0$ if $|\xi| \geq 2^{k+2}$;
  \item $\widehat{\Theta_k}(\xi) = 1$ if $|\xi| \leq 2^{k+1}$;
  \item $\widehat{\psi_{j+1}}(\xi) = 0$ if $|\xi| \leq 2^j$;
  \item $\widehat{\Theta_{k+1}} \widehat{\Theta_k} = \widehat{\Theta_k}$,
    $\widehat{\Theta_j} \widehat{\psi_j} = \widehat{\psi_j}$, and
    $\widehat{\psi_j} \widehat{\Theta_k} = 0$ if $j \geq k + 3$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The formulas follow from \Cref{lem:fourier-dilate}, with
$\widehat{\psi_{j+1}} = \widehat{\Phi_{j+1}} - \widehat{\Phi_j}$, and (1)--(3) from the
properties of the cut-offs; for (3), $\chi_{1,2}(2^{-j-1} \xi) = \chi_{1,2}(2^{-j} \xi) = 1$ if
$|\xi| \leq 2^j$. For (4): where $\widehat{\Theta_k}(\xi) \neq 0$, $|\xi| < 2^{k+2}$ and
$\widehat{\Theta_{k+1}}(\xi) = 1$; where $\widehat{\psi_j}(\xi) \neq 0$, $|\xi| < 2^{j+1}$ and
$\widehat{\Theta_j}(\xi) = 1$; where $\widehat{\Theta_k}(\xi) \neq 0$ and $j \geq k + 3$,
$|\xi| < 2^{k+2} \leq 2^{j-1}$ and $\widehat{\psi_j}(\xi) = 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Kernel identities by Fourier inversion]
\label{lem:conv-of-fourier}
\lean{Rd.conv_eq_of_fourier}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv}
Let $K, L, M \in L_1(V)$, with $L$ bounded and continuous, $M$ continuous and $\widehat M$
integrable. If $\widehat K \widehat L = \widehat M$, then $K * L = M$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$K * L$ is integrable and continuous, and $\widehat{K * L} = \widehat K \widehat L = \widehat M$ is
integrable. The inversion theorem for continuous integrable functions with integrable Fourier
transform gives $K * L = \mathcal F^{-1} \widehat{K * L} = \mathcal F^{-1} \widehat M = M$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Kernel identities]
\label{thm:kernel-identities}
\lean{Rd.conv_Theta_succ, Rd.conv_Theta_psi, Rd.conv_psi_Theta}
\leanok
\uses{lem:conv-of-fourier, lem:lp-kernels-frequency, lem:lp-kernels-basic}
For all $j, k \in \N$, $\Theta_{k+1} * \Theta_k = \Theta_k$ and $\Theta_j * \psi_j = \psi_j$,
and $\psi_j * \Theta_k = 0$ if $j \geq k + 3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Combine \Cref{lem:conv-of-fourier} with part (4) of \Cref{lem:lp-kernels-frequency}.
\end{proof}

\section{Band-limited approximation}

\begin{definition}[Band-limited classes]
\label{def:band-space}
\lean{Rd.bandSpace, Rd.mem_bandSpace, Rd.bandScheme, Rd.mem_bandScheme_two_pow}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-kernels, lem:conv-op, def:comap}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$. The band-limited class of level $k \in \N$ is
$E_k = \{ g \in L_p : \Theta_k * g = g \}$. The band-limited scheme has $A_0 = \{0\}$ and
$A_n = E_{\lfloor \log_2 n \rfloor}$ for $n \geq 1$.
\end{definition}

On the Fourier side $\Theta_k * g = g$ says $\widehat g = \widehat{\Theta_k} \widehat g$: the
spectrum of $g$ lies in the ball of radius $2^{k+2}$, and every $g$ with spectrum in the ball of
radius $2^{k+1}$ belongs to $E_k$. These are the functions of exponential type of
\cite{Nikolskii}, described by a kernel, so that the definition works in every $L_p$ without
distributions.

\begin{lemma}[The band-limited classes]
\label{lem:band-space}
\lean{Rd.smul_mem_bandSpace, Rd.isClosed_bandSpace, Rd.bandSpace_mono}
\leanok
\uses{def:band-space, thm:kernel-identities}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$. Every $E_k$ is a closed complex linear subspace of $L_p$, and
$E_k \subseteq E_{k+1}$ for every $k \in \N$. The band-limited scheme is an approximation scheme
with $A_{2^k} = E_k$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$E_k$ is the kernel of $T_{\Theta_k} - I$. For $g \in E_k$,
$\Theta_{k+1} * g = \Theta_{k+1} * (\Theta_k * g) = (\Theta_{k+1} * \Theta_k) * g
= \Theta_k * g = g$ by \Cref{lem:conv-op} and \Cref{thm:kernel-identities}. The last claim is
\Cref{def:comap}.
\end{proof}

\section{The Littlewood--Paley decomposition}

\begin{definition}[Littlewood--Paley blocks]
\label{def:lp-blocks}
\lean{Rd.lpBlock, Rd.littlewoodPaleyNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-kernels, lem:conv-op, def:lq-norm}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. The Littlewood--Paley blocks of
$f \in L_p$ are $\Delta_j f = \psi_j * f$, $j \in \N$, and the Littlewood--Paley quasi-norm is
$\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} = \|(2^{js} \|\Delta_j f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Bounds and reconstruction]
\label{lem:lp-blocks}
\lean{Rd.enorm_lpBlock_le, Rd.lpBlock_succ, Rd.sum_lpBlock, Rd.tendsto_sum_lpBlock,
  Rd.hasSum_lpBlock}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-blocks, lem:lp-kernels-basic, thm:approx-identity}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. For all $j, J \in \N$,
$\|\Delta_j f\|_p \leq 2 \|\Phi\|_1 \|f\|_p$ and $\sum_{j=0}^J \Delta_j f = \Phi_J * f$, and
$\sum_{j=0}^J \Delta_j f \to f$ in $L_p$ as $J \to \infty$. If $s > 0$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$
and $\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} < \infty$, then $\sum_j \Delta_j f$ converges absolutely to $f$
in $L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The bound is \Cref{lem:conv-op} with $\|\psi_j\|_1 \leq 2 \|\Phi\|_1$, the sum telescopes by
\Cref{lem:lp-kernels-basic}, and $\Phi_J * f \to f$ by \Cref{thm:approx-identity}. For the last
claim, $\|\Delta_j f\|_p \leq 2^{-js} \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q}$ is summable.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Blocks and band-limited functions]
\label{lem:lp-blocks-band}
\lean{Rd.lpBlock_mem_bandSpace, Rd.lpBlock_eq_zero_of_mem, Rd.enorm_lpBlock_le_approxSeq}
\leanok
\uses{lem:lp-blocks, lem:band-space, thm:kernel-identities}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $f \in L_p$ and $i, j \in \N$. Then $\Delta_j f \in E_j$. If
$j \geq i + 3$, then $\Delta_j g = 0$ for every $g \in E_i$, and
$\|\Delta_j f\|_p \leq 2 \|\Phi\|_1 \inf_{g \in E_i} \|f - g\|_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\Theta_j * (\psi_j * f) = (\Theta_j * \psi_j) * f = \psi_j * f$ and
$\psi_j * g = \psi_j * (\Theta_i * g) = (\psi_j * \Theta_i) * g = 0$ by
\Cref{thm:kernel-identities}. Hence $\Delta_j f = \Delta_j (f - g)$ for $g \in E_i$, and
\Cref{lem:lp-blocks} bounds it by $2 \|\Phi\|_1 \|f - g\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Band-limited approximation and Littlewood--Paley blocks]
\label{thm:lp-band}
\lean{Rd.approxNormDyadic_bandScheme_le, Rd.littlewoodPaleyNorm_le_approxNormDyadic,
  Rd.memApprox_bandScheme_iff}
\leanok
\uses{lem:lp-blocks, lem:lp-blocks-band, thm:block-decomposition, def:dyadic-norm, lem:lq-basic}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, and let $A$ be the band-limited
scheme. For every $f \in L_p$,
\[
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_q} \leq 2 (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} \, \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q}
  \quad \text{and} \quad
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} \leq 8 \cdot 8^s \, \|\Phi\|_1 \, \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_q},
\]
and $f \in A^s_q$ if and only if $\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:lp-blocks-band}, $\Delta_j f \in E_j = A_{2^j}$, and by \Cref{lem:lp-blocks} a
finite Littlewood--Paley quasi-norm makes $f = \sum_j \Delta_j f$ a block decomposition, so
\Cref{thm:block-decomposition} gives the first inequality. For the second, split off the first
three terms (\Cref{lem:lq-basic}): $2^{js} \|\Delta_j f\|_p \leq 8^s \cdot 2 \|\Phi\|_1 \|f\|_p$
for $j < 3$, and $2^{(j+3)s} \|\Delta_{j+3} f\|_p \leq 8^s \cdot 2 \|\Phi\|_1 \, 2^{js}
E_{2^j}(f)$ by \Cref{lem:lp-blocks-band}.
\end{proof}

With the Besov space $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(V)$ defined through Littlewood--Paley blocks,
\Cref{thm:lp-band} identifies it with the approximation space of the band-limited scheme.

\section{Differences and moduli of smoothness}

\begin{definition}[Differences and the modulus of smoothness]
\label{def:rd-modulus}
\lean{Rd.fwdDiff_eq_sub, Rd.iterDiff_apply, Rd.modulus, Rd.lpModulus}
\leanok
For $f : V \to \C$, $h \in V$ and $r \in \N$ let $\Delta_h f = f(\cdot + h) - f$ and
$\Delta_h^r = (\Delta_h)^r$, so that
$\Delta_h^r f(x) = \sum_{l=0}^r (-1)^{r-l} \binom{r}{l} f(x + l h)$. For $1 \leq p < \infty$ and
$t \in \R$, the modulus of smoothness is
$\omega_r(f, t)_p = \sup_{\|h\| \leq t} \|\Delta_h^r f\|_p$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Properties of differences]
\label{lem:rd-differences}
\lean{Rd.iterDiff_comp_add, Rd.fwdDiff_nsmul, Rd.aestronglyMeasurable_comp_add,
  Rd.eLpNorm_comp_add_right, Rd.ae_comp_add_right, Rd.aestronglyMeasurable_iterDiff,
  Rd.iterDiff_congr_ae, Rd.eLpNorm_iterDiff_le, Rd.integrable_iterDiff, Rd.conv_comp_add,
  Rd.iterDiff_conv_ae, Rd.iterDiff_dilate}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus, lem:conv-assoc, lem:dilate}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $f \in L_p$, $K \in L_1$, $\Phi : V \to \C$, $h, c \in V$, $m, r \in \N$
and $\lambda > 0$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $\Delta_h^r (f(\cdot + c)) = (\Delta_h^r f)(\cdot + c)$ and
    $\Delta_{mh} f = \sum_{i<m} (\Delta_h f)(\cdot + i h)$;
  \item $\|f(\cdot + c)\|_p = \|f\|_p$ and $\|\Delta_h^r f\|_p \leq 2^r \|f\|_p$;
  \item $\Delta_h^r K$ is integrable, and $\Delta_h^r (K * f) = (\Delta_h^r K) * f$ almost
    everywhere;
  \item $\Delta_h^r (\Phi_\lambda) = (\Delta_{\lambda h}^r \Phi)_\lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Parts (1) and (4) follow by induction on $r$, and (2) holds since translations preserve Lebesgue
measure. For (3), $(K * f)(x + c) = (K(\cdot + c) * f)(x)$ by the substitution $y \mapsto y + c$,
and \Cref{lem:conv-assoc} gives the linearity in the kernel.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Properties of the modulus]
\label{lem:rd-modulus}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_iterDiff_nsmul_iterDiff_le, Rd.eLpNorm_iterDiff_le_modulus, Rd.modulus_mono,
  Rd.modulus_add_le, Rd.modulus_le, Rd.modulus_congr_ae, Rd.modulus_nat_mul_le,
  Rd.modulus_mul_le, Rd.lpModulus_add_le, Rd.lpModulus_le, Rd.lpModulus_zero}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus, lem:rd-differences}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \in \N$ and $f, g \in L_p$. The modulus $\omega_r(f, t)_p$ increases
in $t$, depends only on the class of $f$ modulo null sets, and satisfies
$\omega_r(f + g, t)_p \leq \omega_r(f, t)_p + \omega_r(g, t)_p$ and
$\omega_r(f, t)_p \leq 2^r \|f\|_p$. For all $c, t \geq 0$,
\[
  \omega_r(f, c t)_p \leq (1 + c)^r \, \omega_r(f, t)_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For an integer $m \geq 1$, part (1) of \Cref{lem:rd-differences} gives
$\Delta_{mh}^{a+1} \Delta_h^b f = \sum_{i<m} (\Delta_{mh}^a \Delta_h^{b+1} f)(\cdot + i h)$, and
induction on $a$ yields $\|\Delta_{mh}^r f\|_p \leq m^r \|\Delta_h^r f\|_p$, so
$\omega_r(f, m t)_p \leq m^r \omega_r(f, t)_p$. For real $c$ take
$m = \lfloor c \rfloor + 1 \leq 1 + c$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moduli of convolutions]
\label{lem:rd-modulus-conv}
\lean{Rd.lpModulus_convLp_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:rd-differences, thm:young}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r, k \in \N$, $B \geq 0$ and $K \in L_1$ with
$\|\Delta_v^r K\|_1 \leq B$ for all $\|v\| \leq 2^{-k}$. Then
$\omega_r(K * h, 2^{-k})_p \leq B \, \|h\|_p$ for every $h \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\Delta_v^r (K * h) = (\Delta_v^r K) * h$ almost everywhere by \Cref{lem:rd-differences}, and
Young's inequality gives $\|\Delta_v^r (K * h)\|_p \leq \|\Delta_v^r K\|_1 \|h\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\section{Moduli of smoothness on subsets}

\begin{definition}[The modulus of smoothness on a subset]
\label{def:rd-modulus-on}
\lean{Rd.stepSet, Rd.stepSet_subset, Rd.stepSet_univ, Rd.modulusOn, Rd.lpModulusOn}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus}
For $U \subseteq V$, $h \in V$ and $r \in \N$ let
$U_{rh} = \{x \in V : x, x + h, \dots, x + rh \in U\}$, so that $U_{rh} \subseteq U$ and
$V_{rh} = V$. For $f : V \to \C$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $t \in \R$, the modulus of smoothness
on $U$ is
\[
  \omega_r(f, t, U)_p = \sup_{\|h\| \leq t} \|\Delta_h^r f\|_{L_p(U_{rh})} .
\]
\end{definition}

This is the modulus of \cite[(2.1)]{DeVoreSharpley}, written with the restricted differences
$\Delta_h^r(f, x, U) = \Delta_h^r f(x)$ for $x \in U_{rh}$ and $\Delta_h^r(f, x, U) = 0$ otherwise.

\begin{lemma}[Properties of the modulus on a subset]
\label{lem:rd-modulus-on}
\lean{Rd.indicator_stepSet_iterDiff_indicator, Rd.measurableSet_stepSet, Rd.stepSet_mono,
  Rd.eLpNorm_indicator_iterDiff_le_modulusOn, Rd.modulusOn_mono, Rd.modulusOn_univ,
  Rd.modulusOn_indicator, Rd.modulusOn_add_le, Rd.modulusOn_le_modulus, Rd.modulusOn_le,
  Rd.modulusOn_congr_ae, Rd.modulusOn_mono_set, Rd.lpModulusOn_succ_le,
  Rd.lpModulusOn_le_lpModulus, Rd.lpModulusOn_mono_set, Rd.lpModulusOn_congr}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on, lem:rd-modulus}
Let $U \subseteq V$ be measurable, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \in \N$ and $f, g \in L_p$. Then
$\omega_r(f, t, U)_p$ increases in $t$, depends only on the class of $f|_U$ modulo null sets,
and satisfies $\omega_r(f + g, t, U)_p \leq \omega_r(f, t, U)_p + \omega_r(g, t, U)_p$,
\[
  \omega_r(f, t, U)_p \leq \omega_r(f, t)_p
  \qquad\text{and}\qquad
  \omega_r(f, t, U)_p \leq 2^r \|f\|_{L_p(U)} .
\]
Moreover $\omega_r(f, t, V)_p = \omega_r(f, t)_p$, and
$\omega_r(f, t, U)_p \leq \omega_r(f, t, O)_p$ for measurable $U \subseteq O$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $x \in U_{rh}$ all points $x + lh$, $l \leq r$, lie in $U$, so $\Delta_h^r (1_U f) = \Delta_h^r f$
on $U_{rh}$. Hence $\omega_r(f, t, U)_p = \omega_r(1_U f, t, U)_p \leq \omega_r(1_U f, t)_p \leq
2^r \|1_U f\|_p$ by \Cref{lem:rd-modulus}; the other statements follow from the definition.
\end{proof}

\section{Moment kernels}

\begin{lemma}[The coefficients of the moment kernels]
\label{lem:moment-coefficients}
\lean{Rd.jacksonCoeff, Rd.neg_one_pow_mul_neg_one_pow_sub, Rd.neg_one_pow_mul_sum_iterDiff_coeff,
  Rd.sum_jacksonCoeff}
\leanok
Let $L \in \N$ and $c_l = (-1)^{l+1} \binom{L}{l}$ for $1 \leq l \leq L$. For all
$F_0, \dots, F_L \in \C$,
\[
  (-1)^L \sum_{l=0}^L (-1)^{L-l} \binom{L}{l} F_l = F_0 - \sum_{l=1}^L c_l F_l ,
\]
and $\sum_{l=1}^L c_l = 1$ for $L \geq 1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$(-1)^L (-1)^{L-l} = (-1)^l$ for $l \leq L$, and $\sum_{l=0}^L (-1)^l \binom{L}{l} = (1 - 1)^L = 0$
for $L \geq 1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Moment kernels]
\label{def:moment-kernel}
\lean{Rd.momentKernel, Rd.dilate_dilate, Rd.dilate_momentKernel, Rd.integrable_momentKernel,
  Rd.integral_momentKernel, Rd.lintegral_enorm_ne_zero_of_integral_eq_one}
\leanok
\uses{lem:moment-coefficients, lem:dilate}
Let $g \in L_1$ with $\int_V g = 1$ and $L \in \N$. The moment kernel of order $L$ is
\[
  m = \sum_{l=1}^L (-1)^{l+1} \binom{L}{l} \, g_{1/l} .
\]
Its dilations are $m_c = \sum_{l=1}^L (-1)^{l+1} \binom{L}{l} \, g_{c/l}$ for $c > 0$, and
$\int_V m = 1$ for $L \geq 1$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Averaged differences]
\label{lem:averaged-differences}
\lean{Rd.conv_finsetSum_kernel_ae, Rd.ae_sub_sum_conv_dilate_eq,
  Rd.ae_sub_conv_dilate_momentKernel_eq}
\leanok
\uses{def:moment-kernel, lem:conv-assoc, def:rd-modulus}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $g \in L_1$ with $\int_V g = 1$, $L \in \N$, $c > 0$ and $f \in L_p$.
For almost every $x \in V$,
\[
  f(x) - (m_c * f)(x) = (-1)^L \int_V g(z) \, \Delta^L_{-c^{-1} z} f(x) \, dz .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Write $(g_{c/l} * f)(x) = \int_V g(z) f(x - l c^{-1} z) \, dz$ and $f(x) = \int_V g(z) f(x) \, dz$,
and apply \Cref{lem:moment-coefficients} with $F_l = f(x - l c^{-1} z)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Moment kernels approximate with the modulus]
\label{thm:moment-approx}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_indicator_sub_convLp_momentKernel_le, Rd.enorm_sub_convLp_momentKernel_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:averaged-differences, lem:weighted-jensen, lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $L \in \N$, $c > 0$, and let $g \in L_1$ with $\int_V g = 1$ vanish
outside the ball of radius $R$. Let $U, T \subseteq V$ with $U$ measurable and $U \subseteq T_{Lh}$
for every $h = -c^{-1} z$ with $g(z) \neq 0$. Then for every $f \in L_p$,
\[
  \|f - m_c * f\|_{L_p(U)} \leq \|g\|_1 \, \omega_L(f, R/c, T)_p ,
\]
and in particular $\|f - m_c * f\|_p \leq \|g\|_1 \, \omega_L(f, R/c)_p$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:averaged-differences},
$1_U (f - m_c * f) = (-1)^L \int_V g(z) \, 1_U \Delta^L_{-c^{-1} z} f \, dz$ almost everywhere,
and $|1_U \Delta^L_h f| \leq |1_{T_{Lh}} \Delta^L_h f|$ for the steps $h = -c^{-1} z$ with
$g(z) \neq 0$, which have length at most $R/c$. The weighted Jensen inequality
(\Cref{lem:weighted-jensen}) gives
$\|f - m_c * f\|_{L_p(U)}^p \leq \|g\|_1^{p-1} \int_V |g(z)| \, \omega_L(f, R/c, T)_p^p \, dz$.
The case $U = T = V$ is the second statement, by \Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}.
\end{proof}

\section{Jackson's inequality}

\begin{lemma}[The Jackson approximant]
\label{lem:jackson-approx}
\lean{Rd.conv_Theta_dilate_lpKernel, Rd.jacksonScale, Rd.jacksonScale_pos,
  Rd.jacksonScale_le, Rd.jacksonApprox, Rd.jacksonApprox_mem_bandSpace,
  Rd.ae_sub_jacksonApprox_eq}
\leanok
\uses{lem:averaged-differences, lem:lp-kernels-frequency, lem:conv-of-fourier, lem:band-space,
  lem:dilate}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r, k \in \N$ and $f \in L_p$, and let
\[
  J_k f = \sum_{l=1}^r (-1)^{l+1} \binom{r}{l} \, \Phi_{2^k/l} * f .
\]
Then $J_k f \in E_k$, and for almost every $x \in V$,
\[
  f(x) - J_k f(x) = (-1)^r \int_V \Phi(z) \, \Delta^r_{-2^{-k} z} f(x) \, dz .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The transform $\widehat{\Phi_c} = \chi_{1,2}(c^{-1} \cdot)$ vanishes outside the ball of radius $2c$,
and $\widehat{\Theta_k} = 1$ on the ball of radius $2^{k+1}$, so $\Theta_k * \Phi_c = \Phi_c$ for
$c \leq 2^k$ by \Cref{lem:conv-of-fourier}. The identity is \Cref{lem:averaged-differences} for
$g = \Phi$, $L = r$ and $c = 2^k$, since $\int_V \Phi = \widehat{\Phi}(0) = 1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Jackson's inequality]
\label{thm:band-jackson}
\lean{Rd.one_add_pow_le_two_pow_mul, Rd.jacksonConst, Rd.jacksonConst_ne_top,
  Rd.enorm_sub_jacksonApprox_le, Rd.approxSeq_bandScheme_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:jackson-approx, lem:rd-modulus, lem:weighted-jensen, lem:lp-kernels-basic}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $r \in \N$. There is $C < \infty$ with
\[
  \inf_{g \in E_k} \|f - g\|_p \leq \|f - J_k f\|_p \leq C \, \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p
\]
for all $f \in L_p$ and $k \in \N$; one can take
$C^p = \|\Phi\|_1^{p-1} \int_V |\Phi(z)| (1 + |z|)^{rp} \, dz$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:jackson-approx} and \Cref{lem:weighted-jensen},
\[
  \|f - J_k f\|_p^p \leq \|\Phi\|_1^{p-1} \int_V |\Phi(z)| \, \|\Delta^r_{-2^{-k} z} f\|_p^p \, dz ,
\]
and $\|\Delta^r_{-2^{-k} z} f\|_p \leq \omega_r(f, 2^{-k} |z|)_p \leq (1 + |z|)^r
\omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p$ by \Cref{lem:rd-modulus}. The constant is finite, since $\Phi$ is a Schwartz
function and $|\Phi(z)| (1 + |z|)^N$ is integrable for every $N$.
\end{proof}

\section{The inverse inequality}

\begin{lemma}[Differences of Schwartz functions]
\label{lem:schwartz-differences}
\lean{Rd.fwdDiffSchwartz, Rd.coe_fwdDiffSchwartz, Rd.lineDerivOp_fwdDiffSchwartz,
  Rd.iteratedLineDerivOp_fwdDiffSchwartz, Rd.lintegral_enorm_fwdDiff_le,
  Rd.lintegral_enorm_iterDiff_le, Rd.enorm_iteratedLineDerivOp_le,
  Rd.lintegral_enorm_iterDiff_schwartz_le, Rd.lintegral_enorm_iteratedFDeriv_ne_top}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus}
For every Schwartz function $G$ on $V$, $v \in V$ and $r \in \N$,
\[
  \|\Delta_v^r G\|_1 \leq \|v\|^r \int_V \|D^r G(x)\| \, dx < \infty .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By the fundamental theorem of calculus, $G(x + v) - G(x) = \int_0^1 \partial_v G(x + t v) \, dt$,
so $\|\Delta_v G\|_1 \leq \|\partial_v G\|_1$ by Tonelli's theorem and translation invariance. The
difference $\Delta_v$ and the line derivative $\partial_v$ commute and preserve Schwartz
functions; induction gives $\|\Delta_v^r G\|_1 \leq \|\partial_v^r G\|_1$, and
$|\partial_v^r G(x)| \leq \|v\|^r \|D^r G(x)\|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The inverse inequality for band-limited functions]
\label{thm:band-inverse}
\lean{Rd.bandInverseConst, Rd.bandInverseConst_ne_top, Rd.eLpNorm_iterDiff_Theta_le,
  Rd.lpModulus_le_of_mem_bandSpace}
\leanok
\uses{lem:schwartz-differences, lem:rd-differences, lem:rd-modulus, thm:young, def:band-space}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \in \N$ and $C = 2^r + \int_V \|D^r \Theta(x)\| \, dx$. For all
$j, k \in \N$ and $a \in E_j$,
\[
  \omega_r(a, 2^{-k})_p \leq C \, 2^{-\max(k - j, 0) r} \, \|a\|_p .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $k < j$ use $\omega_r(a, t)_p \leq 2^r \|a\|_p$. For $k \geq j$ and $\|h\| \leq 2^{-k}$,
$a = \Theta_j * a$ gives $\Delta_h^r a = (\Delta_h^r \Theta_j) * a$ almost everywhere, and
$\Delta_h^r \Theta_j = (\Delta_{2^j h}^r \Theta)_{2^j}$ (\Cref{lem:rd-differences}). Young's
inequality and \Cref{lem:schwartz-differences} give
$\|\Delta_h^r a\|_p \leq (2^j \|h\|)^r \|D^r \Theta\|_1 \|a\|_p$, and $2^j \|h\| \leq 2^{-(k-j)}$.
\end{proof}

\section{Besov spaces by differences}

\begin{theorem}[Approximation, Littlewood--Paley blocks and differences]
\label{thm:rd-besov}
\lean{Rd.bandModulusData, Rd.memApprox_bandScheme_iff_modulus,
  Rd.littlewoodPaleyNorm_lt_top_iff_modulus}
\leanok
\uses{thm:band-jackson, thm:band-inverse, lem:rd-modulus, def:modulus-data,
  thm:modulus-coincidence, thm:lp-band}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \geq 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item For $0 \leq s < r$, $f$ lies in the approximation space $A^s_q$ of the band-limited
    scheme if and only if $\|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
  \item For $0 < s < r$, $\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} < \infty$ if and only if
    $\|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:band-jackson} and \Cref{thm:band-inverse} make the modulus compatible with the
band-limited scheme, with inverse order $r$; apply \Cref{thm:modulus-coincidence}. Part (2)
combines (1) with \Cref{thm:lp-band}.
\end{proof}

For $0 < s < r$ the Besov space $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(V)$ thus has three equivalent descriptions: by
approximation with band-limited functions, by Littlewood--Paley blocks, and by differences.

\section{Local means}

\begin{definition}[Local means]
\label{def:local-means}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump, Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport, Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.contDiff_momentKernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.momentKernel_eq_zero,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport_momentKernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.momentSchwartz,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.derivNorm, Rd.LocalMeansBump.scaled, Rd.LocalMeansBump.kernel,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.dual, Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable_scaled,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable_kernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable_dual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.localMean, Rd.LocalMeansBump.localMeansNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:moment-kernel, lem:conv-op}
Let $g : V \to \C$ be smooth with $\int_V g = 1$ and $g = 0$ outside the ball of radius $R$, let
$L \geq 1$ and let $m$ be the moment kernel of order $L$. The local means kernels and the dual
kernels are
\[
  k_0 = \eta_0 = m, \qquad k_j = m_{2^j} - m_{2^{j-1}}, \qquad \eta_j = m_{2^j} + m_{2^{j-1}}
  \qquad (j \geq 1) .
\]
For $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s \in \R$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$, the local means
quasi-norm is $\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The reproducing formula]
\label{lem:reproducing-formula}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_dual_localMean, Rd.LocalMeansBump.tendsto_sum_dual_localMean}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:conv-op, lem:conv-assoc, thm:approx-identity}
With the notation of \Cref{def:local-means}, for every $J \in \N$ and $f \in L_p$,
\[
  \sum_{j=0}^J \eta_j * k_j * f = m_{2^J} * m_{2^J} * f ,
\]
and $\sum_{j=0}^J \eta_j * k_j * f \to f$ in $L_p$ as $J \to \infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The convolution operators commute (\Cref{lem:conv-op}), so
$T_{\eta_j} T_{k_j} = T_{m_{2^j}}^2 - T_{m_{2^{j-1}}}^2$ for $j \geq 1$, from
$a^2 - b^2 = (a + b)(a - b)$, and the sum telescopes. Since $m_{2^J} * f \to f$
(\Cref{thm:approx-identity}) and $\|m_{2^J} * h\|_p \leq \|m\|_1 \|h\|_p$, also
$m_{2^J} * m_{2^J} * f \to f$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local means are bounded by the modulus]
\label{lem:local-means-upper}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_localMean_zero_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_localMean_succ_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.upperConst, Rd.LocalMeansBump.upperConst_ne_top,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.localMeansNorm_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, thm:moment-approx, lem:rd-modulus}
With the notation of \Cref{def:local-means}, for $f \in L_p$ and $j \in \N$,
$\|k_0 * f\|_p \leq \|m\|_1 \|f\|_p$ and
\[
  \|k_{j+1} * f\|_p \leq 2 \|g\|_1 (1 + R)^L \, \omega_L(f, 2^{-j})_p .
\]
Hence for all $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ there is $C < \infty$ with
\[
  \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq C \bigl( \|f\|_p + \|(2^{js} \omega_L(f, 2^{-j})_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\]
for every $f \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$k_{j+1} * f = (f - m_{2^j} * f) - (f - m_{2^{j+1}} * f)$, and \Cref{thm:moment-approx} bounds
both terms by $\|g\|_1 \omega_L(f, R 2^{-j})_p \leq \|g\|_1 (1 + R)^L \omega_L(f, 2^{-j})_p$
(\Cref{lem:rd-modulus}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moduli of the reproducing terms]
\label{lem:dual-kernel-moduli}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_scaled_one, Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_dual_one_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_iterDiff_scaled_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_iterDiff_scaled_le',
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.dualConst, Rd.LocalMeansBump.dualConst_ne_top,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_iterDiff_scaled_le_dyadic,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_iterDiff_dual_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.lpModulus_dual_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:schwartz-differences, lem:rd-differences, lem:rd-modulus-conv}
With the notation of \Cref{def:local-means}, $\|\eta_j\|_1 \leq 2 \|m\|_1$, and there is
$C < \infty$ with
\[
  \omega_L(\eta_j * h, 2^{-i})_p \leq C \, \min\bigl(1, 2^{-(i - j) L}\bigr) \, \|h\|_p
\]
for all $i, j \in \N$ and $h \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The moment kernel $m$ is smooth with compact support, so
$\|\Delta_v^L m_{2^j}\|_1 \leq (2^j \|v\|)^L \|D^L m\|_1$ by \Cref{lem:rd-differences} and
\Cref{lem:schwartz-differences}, and $\|\Delta_v^L m_{2^j}\|_1 \leq 2^L \|m\|_1$. For
$\|v\| \leq 2^{-i}$ this gives $\|\Delta_v^L \eta_j\|_1 \leq C \min(1, 2^{-(i-j)L})$, and
\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-conv} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[An almost diagonal estimate]
\label{lem:almost-diagonal}
\lean{ENNReal.lqNorm_almostDiagonal_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:hardy}
Let $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $0 < s < L$, and let $c_{ij} = 2^{-(i-j)L}$ for $j \leq i$ and
$c_{ij} = 1$ for $j > i$. For every sequence $a$ in $[0, \infty]$,
\[
  \Bigl\| \Bigl( 2^{is} \sum_j c_{ij} \, a_j \Bigr)_i \Bigr\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq \bigl( (1 - 2^{-(L-s)})^{-1} + (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} \bigr) \, \|(2^{js} a_j)_j\|_{\ell_q} .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Split the sum at $j = i$: the part $j \leq i$ is a partial sum with the weights $2^{-i(L-s)}$,
the part $j > i$ a tail sum with the weights $2^{is}$, and \Cref{lem:hardy} bounds both.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The synthesis estimate]
\label{lem:local-means-synthesis}
\lean{Rd.le_tsum_of_tendsto_sum, Rd.LocalMeansBump.synthConst,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.synthConst_ne_top, Rd.LocalMeansBump.modNorm_le_of_tendsto,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.modNorm_le_localMeansNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dual-kernel-moduli, lem:almost-diagonal, lem:reproducing-formula, lem:rd-modulus}
With the notation of \Cref{def:local-means}, let $0 < s < L$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. There is
$C < \infty$ such that for all $h_j \in L_p$ and $F \in L_p$ with
$\sum_{j=0}^J \eta_j * h_j \to F$ in $L_p$,
\[
  \|F\|_p + \|(2^{is} \omega_L(F, 2^{-i})_p)_i\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq C \, \|(2^{js} \|h_j\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} .
\]
In particular $\|f\|_p + \|(2^{is} \omega_L(f, 2^{-i})_p)_i\|_{\ell_q}
\leq C \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$ for every $f \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The norm and the modulus $\omega_L(\cdot, 2^{-i})_p$ are subadditive and bounded by multiples of
$\|\cdot\|_p$, so they pass to the series:
$\omega_L(F, 2^{-i})_p \leq \sum_j \omega_L(\eta_j * h_j, 2^{-i})_p \leq C \sum_j c_{ij}
\|h_j\|_p$ by \Cref{lem:dual-kernel-moduli}, and \Cref{lem:almost-diagonal} bounds the
$\ell_q$ norm. Likewise $\|F\|_p \leq 2 \|m\|_1 \sum_j \|h_j\|_p \leq 2 \|m\|_1 (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1}
\|(2^{js} \|h_j\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$. The case $h_j = k_j * f$ is \Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Local means and differences]
\label{thm:local-means}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.besov_iff_localMeans,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.memApprox_bandScheme_iff_localMeans}
\leanok
\uses{lem:local-means-upper, lem:local-means-synthesis, thm:rd-besov}
With the notation of \Cref{def:local-means}, let $0 < s < L$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and
$f \in L_p$. Then
\[
  \|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty
  \iff \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} < \infty ,
\]
and both hold if and only if $f$ lies in the approximation space $A^s_q$ of the band-limited
scheme.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:local-means-upper} and \Cref{lem:local-means-synthesis}; the last statement is
\Cref{thm:rd-besov}.
\end{proof}

Every estimate runs through the averaged differences of \Cref{lem:averaged-differences}, applied
to $f$ or to the smooth kernels. With \Cref{thm:rd-besov}, local means are a fourth description of
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(V)$ for $0 < s < L$.

\section{Lipschitz graph domains}

\begin{definition}[Lipschitz graph domains]
\label{def:lipschitz-graph}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph, Rd.LipschitzGraph.domain, Rd.LipschitzGraph.cone,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_phi, Rd.LipschitzGraph.isOpen_domain,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.measurableSet_domain}
\leanok
Let $e \in V$ be a unit vector, $M \geq 0$, and $\varphi : V \to \R$ with
$|\varphi(x) - \varphi(y)| \leq M \|x - y\|$ and $\varphi(x + t e) = \varphi(x)$ for all
$x, y \in V$ and $t \in \R$. The Lipschitz graph domain and its cone are
\[
  \Omega = \{x \in V : \varphi(x) < \langle x, e \rangle\}, \qquad
  K = \{y \in V : M \|y - \langle y, e \rangle e\| < \langle y, e \rangle\} .
\]
The set $\Omega$ is open.
\end{definition}

For $V = \R^d$ and $e = e_d$ this is $\Omega = \{x : x_d > \varphi(x_1, \dots, x_{d-1})\}$.

\begin{lemma}[The cone]
\label{lem:graph-cone}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.add_mem, Rd.LipschitzGraph.smul_mem_cone,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.add_nsmul_mem, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneRadius,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneRadius_pos, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneRadius_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.two_mul_add_one_mul_coneRadius_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.ball_subset_cone}
\leanok
\uses{def:lipschitz-graph}
$\Omega + K \subseteq \Omega$ and $c K \subseteq K$ for $c > 0$, so $x + l h \in \Omega$ for
$x \in \Omega$, $h \in K$ and $l \in \N$. The cone $K$ contains the ball of radius
$\rho = 1/(8(M + 1))$ around $e/4$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Write $y = d + \langle y, e \rangle e$ with $d \perp e$. Since $\varphi$ is constant along $e$,
$\varphi(x + y) = \varphi(x + d) \leq \varphi(x) + M \|d\| < \langle x, e \rangle + \langle y, e \rangle$
for $x \in \Omega$ and $y \in K$. For $\|y - e/4\| < \rho$, $\langle y, e \rangle > 1/4 - \rho$ and
$\|y - \langle y, e \rangle e\| < 2\rho$, and $2 M \rho \leq 1/4 - \rho$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Kernels supported in the reflected cone]
\label{def:cone-supported}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.ConeSupported, Rd.LipschitzGraph.ConeSupported.add,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.ConeSupported.sub, Rd.LipschitzGraph.ConeSupported.dilate,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.ConeSupported.finsetSum}
\leanok
\uses{lem:graph-cone, lem:dilate}
A function $k : V \to \C$ is supported in $-K$ if $k(y) \neq 0$ implies $-y \in K$. Linear
combinations of such functions and their dilations $k_c$, $c > 0$, are supported in $-K$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The cone bump]
\label{lem:cone-bump}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneBumpBase, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneBump,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneBump_radius, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_coneBump,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_momentKernel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_scaled,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_kernel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_dual}
\leanok
\uses{lem:graph-cone, def:cone-supported, def:local-means}
There is a smooth $g : V \to \C$ with $\int_V g = 1$ that vanishes outside the ball of radius
$1/2$ and is supported in $-K$. For every $L \geq 1$ the kernels $m_{2^j}$, $k_j$ and $\eta_j$
of \Cref{def:local-means} built from $g$ are supported in $-K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Take the normalised smooth bump of radius $\rho$ around $-e/4$ (\Cref{lem:graph-cone}). It
vanishes outside the ball of radius $1/4 + \rho \leq 1/2$, and $g(y) \neq 0$ gives
$\|y + e/4\| < \rho$, so $-y$ lies in the ball of radius $\rho$ around $e/4$ and in $K$. The kernels are linear combinations of dilations of $g$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Multiplication by an indicator]
\label{lem:indicator-lp}
\lean{Rd.memLp_indicator_coe, Rd.norm_toLp_indicator_le, Rd.indicatorLp,
  Rd.indicatorLp_apply_ae, Rd.enorm_indicatorLp_le}
\leanok
Let $\mu$ be a measure on $X$, $S \subseteq X$ measurable and $1 \leq p \leq \infty$. Then
$h \mapsto 1_S h$ is a bounded linear operator on $L_p(\mu)$ of norm at most $1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$|1_S h| \leq |h|$ pointwise.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The support principle]
\label{lem:support-principle}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.conv_indicator_eq, Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff_apply_ae, Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_cutoff,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_cutoff_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.indicator_ae_eq_of_cutoff_eq,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff_convLp_cutoff, Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff_convLp_eq}
\leanok
\uses{def:cone-supported, lem:graph-cone, lem:indicator-lp, lem:conv-op}
Let $k \in L_1$ be supported in $-K$ and $1 \leq p < \infty$. For $x \in \Omega$ and
$f : V \to \C$, $(k * (1_\Omega f))(x) = (k * f)(x)$. Hence
$1_\Omega (k * (1_\Omega h)) = 1_\Omega (k * h)$ for $h \in L_p$, and
$1_\Omega (k * F) = 1_\Omega (k * f)$ whenever $F, f \in L_p$ agree almost everywhere on
$\Omega$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $k(y) \neq 0$, then $-y \in K$ and $x - y \in x + K \subseteq \Omega$, so
$k(y) (1_\Omega f)(x - y) = k(y) f(x - y)$.
\end{proof}

\section{The extension operator for Lipschitz graph domains}

In this section $\Omega$ is a Lipschitz graph domain, $g$ the bump of \Cref{lem:cone-bump},
$L \geq 1$, and $m$, $k_j$, $\eta_j$ are the kernels of \Cref{def:local-means} built from $g$.
The extension operator is Rychkov's universal extension \cite{Rychkov}, written with these
kernels.

\begin{definition}[Intrinsic local means]
\label{def:intrinsic-local-means}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_congr,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_le_localMeansNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cone-bump, lem:support-principle}
For $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s \in \R$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$, the intrinsic local
means quasi-norm is $\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_j\|_{\ell_q}$. It depends only on the
class of $f|_\Omega$ and is at most the local means quasi-norm
$\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The extension operator]
\label{thm:graph-extension}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.extTerm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.summable_extTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.extension, Rd.LipschitzGraph.tendsto_sum_extTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff_extension, Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_extension_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:intrinsic-local-means, lem:support-principle, lem:reproducing-formula,
  lem:local-means-synthesis}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, and let $f \in L_p$ have a finite
intrinsic local means quasi-norm. Then the series
\[
  \operatorname{Ext} f = \sum_{j=0}^\infty \eta_j * (1_\Omega (k_j * f))
\]
converges absolutely in $L_p$, and $\operatorname{Ext} f = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$. For
$s < L$ there is $C < \infty$, independent of $f$, with
\[
  \|\operatorname{Ext} f\|_p + \|(2^{is} \omega_L(\operatorname{Ext} f, 2^{-i})_p)_i\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq C \, \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_j\|_{\ell_q} .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\|\eta_j * (1_\Omega (k_j * f))\|_p \leq 2 \|m\|_1 \, 2^{-js}
\|(2^{is} \|k_i * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_i\|_{\ell_q}$, which is summable for $s > 0$. By
\Cref{lem:support-principle},
$1_\Omega (\eta_j * (1_\Omega (k_j * f))) = 1_\Omega (\eta_j * k_j * f)$, so on $\Omega$ the
partial sums are those of \Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}, which converge to $f$. The bound is
\Cref{lem:local-means-synthesis} with $h_j = 1_\Omega (k_j * f)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Restrictions by intrinsic local means]
\label{thm:restriction-local-means}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_besov_extension_iff}
\leanok
\uses{thm:graph-extension, def:intrinsic-local-means, lem:local-means-upper}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < L$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. There is
$F \in L_p$ with $F = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$ and
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and only if
$\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_j\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, and then
$F = \operatorname{Ext} f$ is such a function.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $F$ exists, the intrinsic quasi-norm of $f$ equals that of $F$ and is at most the local means
quasi-norm of $F$, which is finite by \Cref{lem:local-means-upper}. The converse is
\Cref{thm:graph-extension}.
\end{proof}

\section{Intrinsic differences on Lipschitz graph domains}

The notation is that of the previous section.

\begin{lemma}[Steps in the cone]
\label{lem:cone-steps}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.subset_stepSet, Rd.LipschitzGraph.subset_stepSet_of_coneSupported,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_cutoff_sub_convLp_scaled_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:graph-cone, def:rd-modulus-on, thm:moment-approx, lem:cone-bump}
For $h \in K$ and $r \in \N$, $\Omega \subseteq \Omega_{rh}$. Consequently, for
$1 \leq p < \infty$, $j \in \N$ and $f \in L_p$,
\[
  \|f - m_{2^j} * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} \leq \|g\|_1 \, \omega_L(f, 2^{-(j+1)}, \Omega)_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The first statement is \Cref{lem:graph-cone}. For $g(z) \neq 0$ the step
$-2^{-j} z = 2^{-j} (-z)$ lies in $K$ and has length at most $2^{-(j+1)}$; apply
\Cref{thm:moment-approx} with $U = T = \Omega$, $R = 1/2$ and $c = 2^j$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Intrinsic local means and moduli on $\Omega$]
\label{lem:intrinsic-upper}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_cutoff_localMean_zero_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_cutoff_localMean_succ_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicConst,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicConst_ne_top, Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cone-steps, lem:support-principle, lem:rd-modulus-on, def:intrinsic-local-means}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. Then
$\|k_0 * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} \leq \|m\|_1 \|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)}$ and
$\|k_{j+1} * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} \leq 2 \|g\|_1 \, \omega_L(f, 2^{-(j+1)}, \Omega)_p$ for
$j \in \N$. Hence, for $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$,
\begin{multline*}
  \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_j\|_{\ell_q} \\
  \leq (\|m\|_1 + 2 \|g\|_1) \bigl( \|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)}
  + \|(2^{js} \omega_L(f, 2^{-j}, \Omega)_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} \bigr) .
\end{multline*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$1_\Omega (k_0 * f) = 1_\Omega (k_0 * (1_\Omega f))$ by \Cref{lem:support-principle}. For
$j \in \N$, $k_{j+1} * f = (f - m_{2^j} * f) - (f - m_{2^{j+1}} * f)$, and \Cref{lem:cone-steps}
bounds both terms by $\|g\|_1 \, \omega_L(f, 2^{-(j+1)}, \Omega)_p$, since the modulus
increases in $t$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Besov spaces on Lipschitz graph domains by intrinsic differences]
\label{thm:graph-besov}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNormOn_le_modNorm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoff_extension_of_modNormOn,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_extension_le_modNormOn,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_besov_extension_iff_modulusOn,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_memApprox_extension_iff}
\leanok
\uses{lem:intrinsic-upper, thm:graph-extension, lem:rd-modulus-on, thm:rd-besov}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. The following are
equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F \in L_p$ with $F = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$ and
    $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F$ in the approximation space $A^s_q$ of the band-limited scheme with
    $F = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$.
\end{enumerate}
For every $F$ as in (2), the quantity in (1) is at most
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$. Under (1), $F = \operatorname{Ext} f$
with $L = r$ satisfies (2), and there is $C < \infty$, independent of $f$, with
\begin{multline*}
  \|\operatorname{Ext} f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(\operatorname{Ext} f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \\
  \leq C \bigl( \|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \bigr) .
\end{multline*}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $F = f$ on $\Omega$, then $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} = \|F\|_{L_p(\Omega)} \leq \|F\|_p$ and
$\omega_r(f, t, \Omega)_p = \omega_r(F, t, \Omega)_p \leq \omega_r(F, t)_p$ by
\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}. Conversely, (1) bounds the intrinsic local means by
\Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper}, and \Cref{thm:graph-extension} gives (2) with the bound for
$\operatorname{Ext} f$. The equivalence of (2) and (3) is \Cref{thm:rd-besov}.
\end{proof}

\section{Pointwise multipliers}

\begin{lemma}[Differences of smooth functions]
\label{lem:smooth-differences}
\lean{Rd.norm_iterDiff_le_of_iteratedFDeriv, Rd.norm_le_of_iteratedFDeriv_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus}
Let $E$ be a normed space, $l \in \N$, $C \geq 0$ and $G : V \to E$ smooth with
$\|D^l G(x)\| \leq C$ for all $x$. Then $\|\Delta_h^l G(x)\| \leq C \|h\|^l$ for all $h, x \in V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induction on $l$: the derivatives of order $l$ of $\Delta_h G$ are $D^l G(\cdot + h) - D^l G$, and
the mean value inequality bounds them by $C \|h\|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Leibniz's rule for differences]
\label{lem:leibniz-differences}
\lean{Rd.sum_choose_mul_succ_add, Rd.iterDiff_mul}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus}
For $u, v : V \to \C$, $h, x \in V$ and $r \in \N$,
\[
  \Delta_h^r (u v)(x) = \sum_{l=0}^r \binom{r}{l} \, \Delta_h^l u(x) \, \Delta_h^{r-l} v(x + l h) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induction on $r$, from $\Delta_h(u v)(x) = \Delta_h u(x) \, v(x + h) + u(x) \, \Delta_h v(x)$ and
Pascal's rule.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The modulus of a product]
\label{lem:product-modulus}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_iterDiff_mul_le, Rd.modulus_mul_smooth_le, Rd.ofReal_one_div_two_pow_pow,
  Rd.memLp_mul_of_bound, Rd.mulLp, Rd.mulLp_apply_ae, Rd.enorm_mulLp_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:smooth-differences, lem:leibniz-differences, lem:rd-differences}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \in \N$, $C \geq 0$ and $\psi : V \to \C$ smooth with
$\|D^l \psi\| \leq C$ for $l \leq r$. For $F \in L_p$, $\psi F \in L_p$ with
$\|\psi F\|_p \leq C \|F\|_p$, and for $t \geq 0$,
\[
  \omega_r(\psi F, t)_p \leq C \sum_{l=0}^r \binom{r}{l} \, t^l \, \omega_{r-l}(F, t)_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:leibniz-differences} and \Cref{lem:smooth-differences},
\[
  |\Delta_h^r(\psi F)(x)| \leq C \sum_l \binom{r}{l} \|h\|^l \, |\Delta_h^{r-l} F(x + l h)| ,
\]
and translations preserve $L_p$ norms.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moduli of lower order]
\label{lem:lower-order-moduli}
\lean{Rd.lqNorm_lpModulus_sub_le, Rd.multiplierConst, Rd.multiplierConst_ne_top,
  Rd.lqNorm_lpModulus_le_multiplierConst}
\leanok
\uses{thm:rd-besov, def:modulus-data, thm:modulus-coincidence}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $0 \leq l \leq r$. There is
$K_l < \infty$ with
\[
  \bigl\| \bigl( 2^{k(s-l)} \omega_{r-l}(F, 2^{-k})_p \bigr)_k \bigr\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq K_l \bigl( \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\]
for every $F \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $1 \leq l \leq s$, both sides are equivalent to the norms of the band-limited approximation
spaces $A^{s-l}_q$ and $A^s_q$ (\Cref{thm:rd-besov} with the orders $r - l$ and $r$), and
$A^s_q \subseteq A^{s-l}_q$. For $l > s$, $\omega_{r-l}(F, t)_p \leq 2^{r-l} \|F\|_p$ and
$\sum_k 2^{k(s-l)q} < \infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise multipliers]
\label{thm:multipliers}
\lean{Rd.modNorm_mulLp_le, Rd.multiplierTotal_ne_top, Rd.modNorm_mulLp_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{lem:product-modulus, lem:lower-order-moduli}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $\psi : V \to \C$ smooth with bounded
derivatives up to order $r$. There is $K < \infty$ with
\[
  \|\psi F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(\psi F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq K \bigl( \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\]
for every $F \in L_p$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:product-modulus}, $2^{ks} \omega_r(\psi F, 2^{-k})_p \leq C \sum_l \binom{r}{l} \,
2^{k(s-l)} \omega_{r-l}(F, 2^{-k})_p$; Minkowski's inequality and \Cref{lem:lower-order-moduli}
bound the $\ell_q$ norm.
\end{proof}

\section{Local extensions}

\begin{lemma}[The support principle for two sets]
\label{lem:support-principle-two}
\lean{Rd.conv_indicator_eq_of_forall, Rd.indicatorLp_convLp_indicatorLp}
\leanok
\uses{lem:conv-op, lem:indicator-lp}
Let $k \in L_1$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $U, W \subseteq V$ measurable with $x - y \in W$ whenever
$x \in U$ and $k(y) \neq 0$. Then $1_U (k * (1_W h)) = 1_U (k * h)$ for every $h \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $x \in U$, $k(y) (1_W h)(x - y) = k(y) h(x - y)$ for every $y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bumps of every radius]
\label{lem:bumps-every-radius}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.rescale, Rd.LocalMeansBump.rescale_radius,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.scaled_eq_zero, Rd.LocalMeansBump.dual_eq_zero,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.kernel_eq_zero, Rd.centeredBumpBase, Rd.LocalMeansBump.centered,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.centered_radius, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_momentKernel_of,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_scaled_of, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_dual_of,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_rescale}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, def:cone-supported, lem:dilate}
If $g$ is a bump for local means of radius $R$ (\Cref{def:local-means}) and $c > 0$, then $g_c$ is
one of radius $R/c$; for every $R > 0$ the normalised smooth bump around $0$ is one of radius $R$.
The kernels $m_{2^j}$, $k_j$ and $\eta_j$ of a bump of radius $R$ vanish outside the ball of radius
$L R$, and they are supported in $-K$ if $g$ is.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\int g_c = \int g$ (\Cref{lem:dilate}); the kernels are linear combinations of dilations
$g_{c'}$ with $c' \geq 1/L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Patches]
\label{lem:patches}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.patch_support, Rd.LipschitzGraph.patch_step,
  Rd.interior_patch_support, Rd.interior_patch_step, Rd.measurableSet_infDist_gt}
\leanok
\uses{lem:graph-cone, lem:bumps-every-radius, def:rd-modulus-on}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R$, $L \in \N$ and $\varepsilon, \delta, \rho > 0$.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item If $\Omega$ is a Lipschitz graph domain, $g$ is supported in $-K$ and $L R \leq \varepsilon$,
    then for $x \in \Omega \cap B(c, \rho - 2\varepsilon)$ and $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$,
    $x - y \in \Omega \cap B(c, \rho - \varepsilon)$, and the steps $h = -c'^{-1} z$, $c' \geq 1$,
    $g(z) \neq 0$, satisfy $\Omega \cap B(c, \rho - \varepsilon) \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for
    $T = \Omega \cap B(c, \rho)$.
  \item If $O \subseteq V$ and $L R \leq \delta$, then for $\operatorname{dist}(x, O^c) > 2\delta$ and
    $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$, $\operatorname{dist}(x - y, O^c) > \delta$, and the same steps satisfy
    $\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, O^c) > \delta\} \subseteq O_{Lh}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The supports of the kernels lie in the ball of radius $L R$ and, in (1), in $-K$; steps in the
cone stay in $\Omega$ (\Cref{lem:graph-cone}), and points within distance $L R$ stay in the larger
balls.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local means on a set]
\label{lem:local-means-on-sets}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.localNormOn, Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_sub_convLp_scaled_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_localMean_zero_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_localMean_succ_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.localNormOn_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:moment-approx, def:local-means, lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R \leq 1/2$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, and let $W, T \subseteq V$ with $W$
measurable and $W \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for the steps $h = -c^{-1} z$, $c \geq 1$, $g(z) \neq 0$. For
$f \in L_p$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$,
\[
  \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(W)})_j\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq (\|m\|_1 + 2 \|g\|_1) \bigl( \|f\|_p + \|(2^{js} \omega_L(f, 2^{-j}, T)_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}
  \bigr) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
As in \Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper}, with \Cref{thm:moment-approx} for the two sets $W$ and $T$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Local extensions]
\label{thm:local-extension}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.localExtTerm, Rd.LocalMeansBump.localExtension,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.summable_localExtTerm, Rd.LocalMeansBump.tendsto_sum_localExtTerm,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.modNorm_localExtension_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.indicatorLp_localExtension,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_local_extension}
\leanok
\uses{lem:support-principle-two, lem:local-means-on-sets, lem:reproducing-formula,
  lem:local-means-synthesis}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R \leq 1/2$, $L \geq 1$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < L$ and
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$. Let $U \subseteq W$ and $U \subseteq T$ be measurable with $x - y \in W$
for $x \in U$ and $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$, and with $W \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for the steps
$h = -c^{-1} z$, $c \geq 1$, $g(z) \neq 0$. If
$\|f\|_{L_p(T)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, T)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, then there is
$F \in L_p$ with $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ and $F = f$
almost everywhere on $U$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Take $F = \sum_j \eta_j * 1_W (k_j * 1_T f)$, which converges by \Cref{lem:local-means-on-sets}
and has the bound of \Cref{lem:local-means-synthesis}. On $U$, \Cref{lem:support-principle-two}
removes the cut-off $1_W$, and the reproducing formula (\Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}) gives
$1_T f = f$.
\end{proof}

The extension operator of \Cref{thm:graph-extension} is the case $U = W = T = \Omega$.

\section{Bounded Lipschitz domains}

\begin{definition}[Bounded Lipschitz domains]
\label{def:bounded-lipschitz}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.inter_ball_eq,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.measurableSet, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.isCompact_closure,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.isCompact_frontier}
\leanok
\uses{def:lipschitz-graph}
A bounded Lipschitz domain is a bounded open set $\Omega \subseteq V$ with finitely many open balls
$B_j = B(c_j, \rho_j)$ with $c_j \in \partial\Omega$, $\partial \Omega \subseteq \bigcup_j B_j$, and
Lipschitz graph domains $\Omega_j$ with $B_j \cap \Omega = B_j \cap \Omega_j$.
\end{definition}

This is the definition of Stein and Triebel, as in \cite[Remark 3.2]{SchneiderVybiral}; the
direction $e_j$ of each graph domain replaces the rotations.

\begin{lemma}[Shrinking and the inner margin]
\label{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_shrink, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_interior_margin}
\leanok
\uses{def:bounded-lipschitz}
There is $\varepsilon > 0$ with $\partial\Omega \subseteq \bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$.
If $\Omega \neq V$, there is $\delta > 0$ with $\operatorname{dist}(x, \Omega^c) > 2\delta$ for every
$x \in \Omega$ outside these balls.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Both by compactness: $\partial\Omega$ is covered by the increasing open sets
$\bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 1/(n+1))$, and $\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c)$ attains a positive
minimum on the compact set $\overline{\Omega} \setminus \bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)
\subseteq \Omega$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Resolutions of unity]
\label{lem:resolution-of-unity}
\lean{Rd.exists_smooth_partition, Rd.exists_iteratedFDeriv_bound}
\leanok
For a closed set $S \subseteq V$ and a finite open cover $(U_i)$ of $S$ there are smooth
$\psi_i \geq 0$ with $\operatorname{supp} \psi_i \subseteq U_i$ and $\sum_i \psi_i = 1$ on $S$.
Smooth functions with compact support have bounded derivatives of every order.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Mathlib's smooth partitions of unity on manifolds, for the model space $V$; the derivatives are
continuous with compact support.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pasting local extensions]
\label{lem:pasting}
\lean{Rd.lpModulus_finsetSum_le, Rd.modNorm_finsetSum_lt_top, Rd.exists_pasting}
\leanok
\uses{lem:resolution-of-unity, thm:multipliers}
Let $O \subseteq V$ be measurable, $(U_i)$ a finite cover of $\overline O$ by bounded open sets, and
$F_i \in L_p$ with $\|F_i\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F_i, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ and
$F_i = f$ almost everywhere on $O \cap U_i$, where $s < r$. Then $f$ agrees on $O$ with some $F$ with
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$F = \sum_i \psi_i F_i$ for a resolution of unity subordinate to $(U_i)$
(\Cref{lem:resolution-of-unity}): each $\psi_i F_i$ has a finite norm by \Cref{thm:multipliers},
and $\psi_i F_i = \psi_i f$ on $O$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Restrictions and smaller sets]
\label{lem:restriction-moduli}
\lean{Rd.modNormOn_le_modNorm_of_indicatorLp, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.modNormOn_mono_set}
\leanok
\uses{lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $O \subseteq V$ be measurable. If $F = f$ almost everywhere on $O$, then
$\|f\|_{L_p(O)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, O)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq
\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$, and the left side decreases when $O$ is
replaced by a measurable subset.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The patches of a bounded Lipschitz domain]
\label{lem:bounded-patches}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_local_extension_boundary,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_local_extension_interior}
\leanok
\uses{thm:local-extension, lem:patches, lem:bumps-every-radius, lem:restriction-moduli}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $\varepsilon, \delta > 0$, and
$f \in L_p$ with $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
For every $j$, $f$ agrees on $\Omega \cap B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$, and on
$\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c) > 2\delta\}$, with functions with finite Besov quasi-norms
on $V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:local-extension} with the patches of \Cref{lem:patches}: for the boundary balls the cone
bump of $\Omega_j$ rescaled to radius $\leq \varepsilon / r$, inside a centred bump of radius
$\leq \delta / r$; the moduli on the smaller sets are bounded by those on $\Omega$
(\Cref{lem:restriction-moduli}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Besov spaces on bounded Lipschitz domains by intrinsic differences]
\label{thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_besov_extension_of_modulusOn,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_besov_extension_iff_modulusOn,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_memApprox_extension_iff}
\leanok
\uses{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry, lem:bounded-patches, lem:pasting, lem:restriction-moduli,
  thm:rd-besov}
Let $\Omega$ be a bounded Lipschitz domain, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$,
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F \in L_p$ with $F = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$ and
    $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F$ in the approximation space $A^s_q$ of the band-limited scheme with $F = f$
    almost everywhere on $\Omega$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
(2) gives (1) by \Cref{lem:restriction-moduli}. For the converse, if $\Omega = V$ take $F = f$.
Otherwise \Cref{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry} gives $\varepsilon$ and $\delta$, the balls
$B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$ and the set $\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c) > 2\delta\}$
cover $\overline\Omega$, \Cref{lem:bounded-patches} gives the local extensions, and
\Cref{lem:pasting} pastes them. (2) and (3) agree by \Cref{thm:rd-besov}.
\end{proof}

\section{Atoms}

Atoms are functions of small support with size bounds at a dyadic scale. As in Schneider and
Vyb\'{\i}ral~\cite{SchneiderVybiral}, they need no moment conditions and no smoothness beyond the
order of the differences that measure them.

\begin{definition}[Dyadic cells]
\label{def:dyadic-cells}
\lean{Rd.gridBasis, Rd.cellIndex, Rd.cell, Rd.cellCenter, Rd.mem_cell_cellIndex,
  Rd.repr_cellCenter, Rd.measurable_cellIndex, Rd.measurableSet_cell}
\leanok
Let $(b_i)$ be an orthonormal basis of $V$. For $j \in \N$ and $m \in \Z^n$ the dyadic cell of
level $j$ and index $m$ is
$P_{j,m} = \{x \in V : \lfloor 2^j \langle b_i, x \rangle \rfloor = m_i \text{ for all } i\}$,
with centre $c_{j,m} = \sum_i (m_i + 1/2) 2^{-j} b_i$. Every $x$ lies in the cell of index
$(\lfloor 2^j \langle b_i, x \rangle \rfloor)_i$, and the cells are measurable.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cells and centres]
\label{lem:dyadic-cells}
\lean{Rd.abs_repr_le_norm, Rd.abs_repr_sub_cellCenter_le, Rd.norm_sub_cellCenter_le,
  Rd.card_le_of_norm_cellCenter_sub_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells}
For $x \in P_{j,m}$ every coordinate of $x - c_{j,m}$ is at most $2^{-j}/2$ in modulus, and
$\|x - c_{j,m}\| \leq n 2^{-j}$. For $\rho \geq 0$ at most $(2\rho + 2)^n$ centres of level $j$ lie
in a closed ball of radius $\rho 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The coordinates of the centres in such a ball lie in intervals of length $2 \rho 2^{-j}$, and such
an interval contains at most $2\rho + 2$ points of $(\Z + 1/2) 2^{-j}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded overlap]
\label{lem:bounded-overlap}
\lean{Rd.exists_finset_of_card_le, Rd.enorm_tsum_rpow_le_of_overlap, Rd.eLpNorm_rpow_le_of_overlap}
\leanok
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and let $g_m$, $m$ in a countable set, be measurable functions such that at
every point at most $N$ of them are nonzero. If $F = \sum_m g_m$ almost everywhere, then
$\|F\|_p^p \leq N^{p-1} \sum_m \|g_m\|_p^p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At every point the sum has at most $N$ nonzero terms, and
$|\sum_{m \in T} a_m|^p \leq |T|^{p-1} \sum_{m \in T} |a_m|^p$ by H\"older's inequality. Integrate
and exchange the sum with the integral.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Regular atoms and the coefficient quasi-norm]
\label{def:regular-atoms}
\lean{Rd.IsRegularAtom, Rd.levelNorm, Rd.levelNorm_mono, Rd.coeffNorm}
\leanok
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $\sigma \geq 0$, $r \in \N$ and $d, C \geq 0$. A regular atom of level
$j$, exponent $\sigma$ and order $r$ with centre $c$ is a measurable $a : V \to \C$ with $a = 0$
outside $\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$,
\[
  \|a\|_p \leq C 2^{-jn/p} \quad\text{and}\quad
  \|\Delta_h^r a\|_p \leq C 2^{-jn/p} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^\sigma) \quad (h \in V).
\]
For coefficients $\lambda = (\lambda_{j,m})$ let
$\|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} = (\sum_m |\lambda_{j,m}|^p)^{1/p}$ and
$\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p})_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

The Lip atoms of \cite[Definition 2.1]{SchneiderVybiral}, with $|a| \leq 1$ and
$|a(x) - a(y)| \leq 2^j |x - y|$, are regular atoms with $\sigma = r = 1$, and their smooth atoms of
\cite[Definition 1.3]{SchneiderVybiral} are regular atoms with $\sigma = r = K$
(\Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms}).

\begin{lemma}[Level sums]
\label{lem:level-sums}
\lean{Rd.iterDiff_tsum_apply, Rd.le_of_rpow_le_overlap, Rd.one_sub_one_div_toReal_nonneg,
  Rd.summable_levelSum, Rd.eLpNorm_levelSum_le, Rd.modulus_levelSum_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:bounded-overlap, def:regular-atoms, lem:rd-modulus}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, and let $a_m$ be regular atoms of level $j$ with centres $c_m$ and
coefficients $\lambda_m$, such that at most $N$ of the centres $c_m$ with $\lambda_m \neq 0$ lie in a
ball of radius $(d + r) 2^{-j}$. Then $f = \sum_m \lambda_m a_m$ converges at every point, and
\[
  \|f\|_p \leq N^{1-1/p} C 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p}, \qquad
  \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p \leq 2^r N^{1-1/p} C 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p} c_{kj},
\]
with $c_{kj} = 2^{-(k-j)\sigma}$ for $j \leq k$ and $c_{kj} = 1$ for $j > k$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At every point at most $N$ of the terms are nonzero, and \Cref{lem:bounded-overlap} applies. For
$j \leq k$ and $\|h\| \leq 2^{-k}$ the differences $\Delta_h^r a_m$ vanish outside
$B(c_m, (d + r) 2^{-j})$, and \Cref{lem:bounded-overlap} applies to
$\Delta_h^r f = \sum_m \lambda_m \Delta_h^r a_m$ with $(2^j \|h\|)^\sigma \leq 2^{-(k-j)\sigma}$; for
$j > k$, $\omega_r(f, t)_p \leq 2^r \|f\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic synthesis]
\label{thm:atomic-synthesis}
\lean{Rd.atomSynthConst, Rd.atomSynthConst_ne_top, Rd.modNorm_le_coeffNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:level-sums, lem:almost-diagonal}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $0 < s < \sigma$. Let $a_{j,m}$ be regular atoms
of level $j$ such that the centres of the atoms with $\lambda_{j,m} \neq 0$ satisfy the overlap
condition of \Cref{lem:level-sums} at every level, and let $F = \sum_j f_j$ in $L_p$ with
$f_j = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere. Then
\[
  \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq K \|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}},
\]
with $K = N^{1-1/p} C \bigl((1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} + 2^r ((1 - 2^{-(\sigma - s)})^{-1} +
(1 - 2^{-s})^{-1})\bigr)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 2.6 and Corollary 2.8, direction $\Leftarrow$]{SchneiderVybiral} for
$p \geq 1$. The norm and the moduli are subadditive and bounded on $L_p$, so they are bounded by the
sums over the levels. With \Cref{lem:level-sums} and $b_j = 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p}$,
the almost diagonal lemma (\Cref{lem:almost-diagonal}) with exponent $\sigma$ sums the levels,
since $2^{js} b_j = 2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Synthesis of one level]
\label{lem:single-level}
\lean{Rd.lqNorm_ite_eq, Rd.isRegularAtom_zero, Rd.modNorm_level_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:atomic-synthesis}
For regular atoms of level $j$ with the overlap of \Cref{lem:level-sums}, $F = \sum_m \lambda_m a_m$
in $L_p$ and $0 < s < \sigma$,
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq K 2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p}$,
with the constant $K$ of \Cref{thm:atomic-synthesis}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:atomic-synthesis} with the coefficients and atoms of the other levels equal to $0$; the
zero function is a regular atom, and a sequence with one nonzero term $x$ has $\ell_q$-norm $x$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Atoms from pointwise bounds]
\label{lem:pointwise-atoms}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_le_of_bound_of_support, Rd.volume_closedBall_div_two_pow,
  Rd.rpow_mul_dyadicWeight_neg, Rd.isRegularAtom_of_pointwise,
  Rd.enorm_iterDiff_le_of_iteratedFDeriv}
\leanok
\uses{def:regular-atoms, lem:smooth-differences}
Let $a$ be measurable with $a = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$, $|a| \leq A$ and
$|\Delta_h^r a| \leq A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^\sigma)$. Then $a$ is a regular atom with the constant
$A ((r + 1) d^n |B_1|)^{1/p}$. A smooth $a$ with $\|D^l a\| \leq A 2^{jl}$ for $l \leq r$ has
$|\Delta_h^r a| \leq 2^r A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^r)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
A function with $|g| \leq B$ that vanishes outside $S$ has $\|g\|_p \leq B |S|^{1/p}$. The
difference $\Delta_h^r a$ vanishes outside $r + 1$ translates of the ball, and
$|\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})| = d^n |B_1| 2^{-jn}$. For smooth $a$, \Cref{lem:smooth-differences}
gives $|\Delta_h^r a| \leq A (2^j \|h\|)^r$, and $|\Delta_h^r a| \leq 2^r \sup |a|$.
\end{proof}

\section{Atomic decompositions}

\begin{lemma}[The cells partition the space]
\label{lem:cells-partition}
\lean{Rd.cell_disjoint, Rd.iUnion_cell, Rd.lintegral_eq_tsum_cell, Rd.eLpNorm_rpow_eq_tsum_cell,
  Rd.card_le_of_norm_sub_cellCenter_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells, lem:dyadic-cells}
The cells of one level are disjoint and cover $V$. Hence $\int F = \sum_m \int_{P_{j,m}} F$ for
$F \geq 0$ and $\|g\|_p^p = \sum_m \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p^p$. At most
$\lceil (2\rho + 2)^n \rceil$ centres of level $j$ lie in a ball of radius $\rho 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Countable additivity of the integral, and \Cref{lem:dyadic-cells}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise bounds of convolutions]
\label{lem:conv-pointwise}
\lean{Rd.lintegral_closedBall_le, Rd.lintegral_kernel_mul_le, Rd.enorm_conv_le_of_bound,
  Rd.integrable_translate_mul, Rd.conv_add_apply, Rd.iterDiff_conv_apply,
  Rd.enorm_iterDiff_le_of_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $g \in L_p$, and $|K| \leq A$ with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho)$.
Then $\int_{\overline{B}(x, \rho)} |g| \leq |\overline{B}(x, \rho)|^{1 - 1/p} \|g\|_p$ and
\[
  |(K * g)(x)| \leq A |\overline{B}(0, \rho)|^{1-1/p} \|g\|_p \quad (x \in V).
\]
For a continuous $K$ with compact support the integrals $\int K(y + v) g(x - y) \, dy$ converge
absolutely, they equal $(K * g)(x + v)$, and $\Delta_h^r (K * g) = (\Delta_h^r K) * g$ at every point.
If $|f| \leq B$, then $|\Delta_h^r f| \leq 2^r B$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
H\"older's inequality on the ball, and the translation invariance of the Lebesgue measure; the
differences are finite sums of translates.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Cell atoms]
\label{def:cell-atoms}
\lean{Rd.cellCoef, Rd.cellAtom, Rd.enorm_cellCoef, Rd.cellCoef_mul_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells}
For $1 \leq p < \infty$, a kernel $K$ and $g \in L_p$, the coefficients of the cells of level $j$
are $\lambda_{j,m} = 2^{jn/p} \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p$, and the cell atoms are
$a_{j,m} = \lambda_{j,m}^{-1} K * (1_{P_{j,m}} g)$, with $a_{j,m} = 0$ if $\lambda_{j,m} = 0$.
Then $\lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m} = K * (1_{P_{j,m}} g)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cell decomposition of a convolution]
\label{lem:cell-decomposition}
\lean{Rd.conv_eq_tsum_cellAtom, Rd.continuous_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{def:cell-atoms, lem:cells-partition, lem:conv-pointwise}
Let $K$ be continuous with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho)$, and $g \in L_p$. Then
$(K * g)(x) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}(x)$ at every $x \in V$, with finitely many nonzero terms,
and the cell atoms are continuous.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Only the finitely many cells that meet $\overline{B}(x, \rho)$ contribute, and on this ball their
indicators add up to $1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cell atoms are atoms]
\label{lem:cell-atoms-regular}
\lean{Rd.cellAtom_eq_zero, Rd.volume_closedBall_div_two_pow_rpow,
  Rd.enorm_inv_cellCoef_mul_conv_le, Rd.enorm_cellAtom_le, Rd.enorm_iterDiff_cellAtom_le,
  Rd.cellAtomConst, Rd.cellAtomConst_ne_top, Rd.isRegularAtom_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cell-decomposition, lem:conv-pointwise, lem:pointwise-atoms}
Let $K$ be continuous with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho 2^{-j})$ and
$|\Delta_h^l K| \leq A 2^{jn} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for $l \in \{0, r\}$. Then the cell atoms of
level $j$ vanish outside $\overline{B}(c_{j,m}, (\rho + n) 2^{-j})$,
\begin{gather*}
  |a_{j,m}| \leq A (\rho^n |B_1|)^{1 - 1/p}, \\
  |\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}| \leq 2^r A ((\rho + r)^n |B_1|)^{1 - 1/p} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^r),
\end{gather*}
so they are regular atoms of order and exponent $r$ with radius factor $\rho + n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:conv-pointwise} with $\lambda_{j,m} = 2^{jn/p} \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p$: the factors
$2^{jn}$, $|\overline{B}(0, \rho 2^{-j})|^{1-1/p} = (\rho^n |B_1|)^{1-1/p} 2^{-jn(1-1/p)}$ and
$2^{-jn/p}$ cancel. For $\|h\| \leq 2^{-j}$ the kernel $\Delta_h^r K$ of $\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}$
vanishes outside $\overline{B}(0, (\rho + r) 2^{-j})$; for larger $h$,
$|\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}| \leq 2^r \sup |a_{j,m}|$. \Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The coefficients of the cells]
\label{lem:cell-coefficients}
\lean{Rd.levelNorm_cellCoef, Rd.coeffNorm_cellCoef}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cells-partition, def:cell-atoms}
$\|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} = 2^{jn/p} \|g\|_p$. Hence the cell coefficients of $g_j \in L_p$
have $\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{js} \|g_j\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cells-partition}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounds of the dual kernels]
\label{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
\lean{Rd.ofReal_two_pow_pow, Rd.LocalMeansBump.scaled_eq_zero_of_lt,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.dual_eq_zero_of_lt, Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport_dual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.continuous_dual, Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_iterDiff_dual_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:smooth-differences, lem:resolution-of-unity, lem:rd-differences}
For a bump $g$ of radius $R$ and $L, r \in \N$, the kernels $m_{2^j}$ and $\eta_j$ are continuous
and vanish outside $\overline{B}(0, L R 2^{-j})$ and $\overline{B}(0, 2 L R 2^{-j})$, and there is
$A < \infty$ with $|\Delta_h^l \eta_j| \leq A 2^{jn} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for all $l \leq r$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The moment kernel is smooth with compact support, so its derivatives are bounded
(\Cref{lem:resolution-of-unity}). \Cref{lem:smooth-differences} bounds its differences, and
$\Delta_h^l (\Phi_c) = (\Delta_{ch}^l \Phi)_c$ for the dilations (\Cref{lem:rd-differences}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The cell atoms of the dual kernels]
\label{lem:cell-atoms-dual}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_cellAtom_pointwise_bound}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cell-atoms-regular, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
For every order $r$ there is $A < \infty$ such that the cell atoms of $\eta_j * g$, $g \in L_p$,
satisfy $|\Delta_h^l a_{j,m}| \leq A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for all $l \leq r$, $j$ and $m$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular} with the bounds of \Cref{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}, for each $l \leq r$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic decomposition]
\label{thm:atomic-decomposition}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.atomic_decomposition}
\leanok
\uses{lem:reproducing-formula, lem:cell-decomposition, lem:cell-atoms-regular,
  lem:cell-coefficients, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $L, r \in \N$. There is $C < \infty$ such
that for every $F \in L_p$ the terms of the reproducing formula $F = \sum_j \eta_j * (k_j * F)$
(\Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}) are $\eta_j * (k_j * F) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost
everywhere, with the cell atoms of $k_j * F$; these are regular atoms of order and exponent $r$
with radius factor $2LR + n$ and constant $C$, and
$\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{js} \|k_j * F\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$ for all $s$ and $q$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cell-decomposition} for the kernels $\eta_j$, whose bounds
(\Cref{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}) make the cell atoms regular (\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular}), and
\Cref{lem:cell-coefficients} for the coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic characterisation of Besov spaces]
\label{thm:atomic-characterisation}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.besov_iff_atomic}
\leanok
\uses{thm:atomic-decomposition, thm:atomic-synthesis, thm:local-means, lem:cells-partition}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $L \geq 1$, $0 < s < L$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $F \in L_p$. Then
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and only if
$F = \sum_j f_j$ in $L_p$ with $f_j = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere, for regular
atoms of order and exponent $L$ whose centres satisfy the overlap condition of
\Cref{lem:level-sums}, and $\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 2.6]{SchneiderVybiral} for $p \geq 1$. If the norm is finite, so is the local
